I didn’t want to make too big a deal of it, but yeah, almost every day since then.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any days when you feel down or lose interest in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I mean, I wouldn’t say I’m really down most days. I still do the things I usually do, I think. Maybe I’m less excited sometimes, but I haven’t really noticed losing interest, if that makes sense. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any days where you don’t take pleasure in things you used to enjoy, even a little bit?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in a way I’ve really noticed. Like, if I do something I usually like, I can still enjoy it some. Maybe I’m a little less into things sometimes, but I wouldn’t want to say I don’t take pleasure in them. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you have significantly less energy or feel fatigued nearly every day, even after resting?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess that’s actually a big part of why I came in. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but I feel pretty worn out almost every day. Even if I get a full night’s sleep, when I get up for my cashier shift, my body and my head still feel like they’re dragging. Does that count as fatigue?\n\nDoctor: Do you find that it’s harder to focus or make decisions these days, even with everyday things?\n\nPatient: Yeah… somewhat. I don’t want to make it sound like I can’t do my job, because I can, but at the register I’ll be looking right at the screen and kind of lose track because my mind goes to stuff at home or bills. Then I’ll have to recount the drawer, or I’ll catch a small mistake and fix it. It’s not constant, but it has been happening. Is that what you mean by focus?\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling like you're not good enough or blamed yourself for things more than usual lately?\n\nPatient: I mean… not really, I don’t think. I’ll get frustrated with myself sometimes, like if I mess something up or feel like I should be handling things better, but I don’t think I’m sitting around feeling worthless or anything like that. At least not more than usual. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite or weight recently—like eating more or less than usual or gaining or losing weight without trying?\n\nPatient: Um… not that I’ve really noticed. My eating’s maybe a little all over the place depending on the day, but I don’t think I’ve had any real weight change or anything like that. I haven’t been trying to lose or gain. Is that okay—like, is “not really” an answer for that?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any days where you don’t take much interest in activities you usually care about, even things like hobbies or time with friends?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not really. I mean, some days I’m tired and I might just want to stay home instead of doing much, but I wouldn’t say I’ve stopped caring about things or friends or anything. I still… you know, I still want to do those things. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have trouble staying asleep—like waking up in the middle of the night and having a hard time falling back asleep?\n\nPatient: Yeah… a little, I guess. I mean, most nights I go to bed pretty tired, but then my mind kind of starts going—bills, my schedule at work, whether everyone at home is okay, stuff like that. I do sleep, but it’s like I don’t fully settle down, and I wake up feeling like I didn’t really rest. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever wake up earlier than planned and then have trouble falling back asleep, even if you’re still tired?\n\nPatient: Not so much waking up early, I don’t think. It’s more like… I get into bed tired, and then I’m just lying there going over bills, or my work schedule, or whether everyone at home is okay. I do sleep, but it doesn’t feel like I ever really settle down, and then I wake up still tired. I’m not sure if that counts as the same thing.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual, or on the flip side, feeling more restless or unable to sit still?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’ve noticed that, no. I mean, I can get a little fidgety sometimes, but I don’t think I’m moving strangely or talking slower than usual. Nobody’s said anything to me about it, at least. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you found it harder to think clearly or concentrate on tasks, even simple ones, lately?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but at the register sometimes I’ll be looking right at the screen and still kind of lose track because I’m thinking about stuff at home or bills. Then I have to recount the drawer or catch a small mistake before it becomes a problem. I’m trying to be careful, but… yeah, concentrating has been a little harder.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Does that count as fatigue?\n\nDoctor: Do you find that it’s harder to focus or make decisions these days, even with everyday things?\n\nPatient: Yeah… somewhat. I don’t want to make it sound like I can’t do my job, because I can, but at the register I’ll be looking right at the screen and kind of lose track because my mind goes to stuff at home or bills. Then I’ll have to recount the drawer, or I’ll catch a small mistake and fix it. It’s not constant, but it has been happening. Is that what you mean by focus?\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling like you're not good enough or blamed yourself for things more than usual lately?\n\nPatient: I mean… not really, I don’t think. I’ll get frustrated with myself sometimes, like if I mess something up or feel like I should be handling things better, but I don’t think I’m sitting around feeling worthless or anything like that. At least not more than usual. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite or weight recently—like eating more or less than usual or gaining or losing weight without trying?\n\nPatient: Um… not that I’ve really noticed. My eating’s maybe a little all over the place depending on the day, but I don’t think I’ve had any real weight change or anything like that. I haven’t been trying to lose or gain. Is that okay—like, is “not really” an answer for that?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any days where you don’t take much interest in activities you usually care about, even things like hobbies or time with friends?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not really. I mean, some days I’m tired and I might just want to stay home instead of doing much, but I wouldn’t say I’ve stopped caring about things or friends or anything. I still… you know, I still want to do those things. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have trouble staying asleep—like waking up in the middle of the night and having a hard time falling back asleep?\n\nPatient: Yeah… a little, I guess. I mean, most nights I go to bed pretty tired, but then my mind kind of starts going—bills, my schedule at work, whether everyone at home is okay, stuff like that. I do sleep, but it’s like I don’t fully settle down, and I wake up feeling like I didn’t really rest. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever wake up earlier than planned and then have trouble falling back asleep, even if you’re still tired?\n\nPatient: Not so much waking up early, I don’t think. It’s more like… I get into bed tired, and then I’m just lying there going over bills, or my work schedule, or whether everyone at home is okay. I do sleep, but it doesn’t feel like I ever really settle down, and then I wake up still tired. I’m not sure if that counts as the same thing.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual, or on the flip side, feeling more restless or unable to sit still?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’ve noticed that, no. I mean, I can get a little fidgety sometimes, but I don’t think I’m moving strangely or talking slower than usual. Nobody’s said anything to me about it, at least. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you found it harder to think clearly or concentrate on tasks, even simple ones, lately?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but at the register sometimes I’ll be looking right at the screen and still kind of lose track because I’m thinking about stuff at home or bills. Then I have to recount the drawer or catch a small mistake before it becomes a problem. I’m trying to be careful, but… yeah, concentrating has been a little harder.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any days where you don’t take much interest in activities you usually care about, even things like hobbies or time with friends, even if you still want to do them?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not really. I mean, some days I’m tired and I might not feel like doing much, but I wouldn’t say I’ve stopped caring about things or anything like that. If I make plans, I usually still want to go… I might just be a little worn out. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any days where you don’t take much interest in activities you usually care about, even things like hobbies or time with friends, even if you still want to do them?\n\nPatient: I guess I’d say no, not really. I still care about seeing people or doing the usual stuff. Sometimes I’m just tired or not as energetic, but the interest is still there. I hope that makes sense.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

Can you tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Hi, yeah… thanks for seeing me. I guess I’ve just been feeling pretty worn out almost every day. It’s not, like, dramatic or anything, but even when I get a full night’s sleep, I wake up and my body and my head still feel like they’re dragging, especially when I have to get up for my cashier shift. I wasn’t sure if that was worth coming in for, but it’s been bothering me.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way almost every day?\n\nPatient: Um… it’s been a while, I guess. Probably about a year and three months now. It kind of started after our older relative moved in with us, if that makes sense. I didn’t want to make too big a deal of it, but yeah, almost every day since then.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any days when you feel down or lose interest in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I mean, I wouldn’t say I’m really down most days. I still do the things I usually do, I think. Maybe I’m less excited sometimes, but I haven’t really noticed losing interest, if that makes sense. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any days where you don’t take pleasure in things you used to enjoy, even a little bit?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in a way I’ve really noticed. Like, if I do something I usually like, I can still enjoy it some. Maybe I’m a little less into things sometimes, but I wouldn’t want to say I don’t take pleasure in them. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you have significantly less energy or feel fatigued nearly every day, even after resting?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess that’s actually a big part of why I came in. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but I feel pretty worn out almost every day. Even if I get a full night’s sleep, when I get up for my cashier shift, my body and my head still feel like they’re dragging. Does that count as fatigue?\n\nDoctor: Do you find that it’s harder to focus or make decisions these days, even with everyday things?\n\nPatient: Yeah… somewhat. I don’t want to make it sound like I can’t do my job, because I can, but at the register I’ll be looking right at the screen and kind of lose track because my mind goes to stuff at home or bills. Then I’ll have to recount the drawer, or I’ll catch a small mistake and fix it. It’s not constant, but it has been happening. Is that what you mean by focus?\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling like you're not good enough or blamed yourself for things more than usual lately?\n\nPatient: I mean… not really, I don’t think. I’ll get frustrated with myself sometimes, like if I mess something up or feel like I should be handling things better, but I don’t think I’m sitting around feeling worthless or anything like that. At least not more than usual. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite or weight recently—like eating more or less than usual or gaining or losing weight without trying?\n\nPatient: Um… not that I’ve really noticed. My eating’s maybe a little all over the place depending on the day, but I don’t think I’ve had any real weight change or anything like that. I haven’t been trying to lose or gain. Is that okay—like, is “not really” an answer for that?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any days where you don’t take much interest in activities you usually care about, even things like hobbies or time with friends?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not really. I mean, some days I’m tired and I might just want to stay home instead of doing much, but I wouldn’t say I’ve stopped caring about things or friends or anything. I still… you know, I still want to do those things. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have trouble staying asleep—like waking up in the middle of the night and having a hard time falling back asleep?\n\nPatient: Yeah… a little, I guess. I mean, most nights I go to bed pretty tired, but then my mind kind of starts going—bills, my schedule at work, whether everyone at home is okay, stuff like that. I do sleep, but it’s like I don’t fully settle down, and I wake up feeling like I didn’t really rest. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever wake up earlier than planned and then have trouble falling back asleep, even if you’re still tired?\n\nPatient: Not so much waking up early, I don’t think. It’s more like… I get into bed tired, and then I’m just lying there going over bills, or my work schedule, or whether everyone at home is okay. I do sleep, but it doesn’t feel like I ever really settle down, and then I wake up still tired. I’m not sure if that counts as the same thing.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual, or on the flip side, feeling more restless or unable to sit still?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’ve noticed that, no. I mean, I can get a little fidgety sometimes, but I don’t think I’m moving strangely or talking slower than usual. Nobody’s said anything to me about it, at least. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you found it harder to think clearly or concentrate on tasks, even simple ones, lately?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but at the register sometimes I’ll be looking right at the screen and still kind of lose track because I’m thinking about stuff at home or bills. Then I have to recount the drawer or catch a small mistake before it becomes a problem. I’m trying to be careful, but… yeah, concentrating has been a little harder.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any days where you don’t take much interest in activities you usually care about, even things like hobbies or time with friends, even if you still want to do them?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not really. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any days where you don’t take much interest in activities you usually care about, even things like hobbies or time with friends, even if you still want to do them?\n\nPatient: I guess I’d say no, not really. I hope that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any days where you don’t take much interest in activities you usually care about, even things like hobbies or time with friends, even if you still want to do them?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’ve really noticed that, no. Is that okay—like, is “not really” an answer for that?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any days where you don’t take much interest in activities you usually care about, even things like hobbies or time with friends?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not really. I mean, some days I’m tired and I might just want to stay home instead of doing much, but I wouldn’t say I’ve stopped caring about things or friends or anything. I still… you know, I still want to do those things. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have trouble staying asleep—like waking up in the middle of the night and having a hard time falling back asleep?\n\nPatient: Yeah… a little, I guess. I mean, most nights I go to bed pretty tired, but then my mind kind of starts going—bills, my schedule at work, whether everyone at home is okay, stuff like that.
